In 1999 more than 11,000 deaths were reported to the office of the <a href="/wiki/International_Criminal_Tribunal_for_the_former_Yugoslavia" title="International Criminal Tribunal for the former Yugoslavia">International Criminal Tribunal for the former Yugoslavia</a> prosecutor <a href="/wiki/Carla_Del_Ponte" title="Carla Del Ponte">Carla Del Ponte</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> As of 2010<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kosovo&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, some 3,000 people were still missing, including 2,500 Albanians, 400 Serbs and 100 <a href="/wiki/Romani_people" title="Romani people">Roma</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> By June, Milošević agreed to a foreign military presence in Kosovo and the withdrawal of his troops. During the <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_War" title="Kosovo War">Kosovo War</a>, over 90,000 Serbian and other non-Albanian refugees fled the province. In the days after the Yugoslav Army withdrew, over 80,000 Serb and other non-Albanian civilians (almost half of 200,000 estimated to live in Kosovo) were expelled from Kosovo, and many of the remaining civilians were victims of abuse.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Wills_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wills-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> After the Kosovo and other <a href="/wiki/Yugoslav_Wars" title="Yugoslav Wars">Yugoslav Wars</a>, Serbia became home to the highest number of refugees and <a href="/wiki/Internally_displaced_person" title="Internally displaced person">IDPs</a> (including Kosovo Serbs) in Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kosovo-metohija-koreni-duse029.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Kosovo-metohija-koreni-duse029.jpg/220px-Kosovo-metohija-koreni-duse029.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="140" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Kosovo-metohija-koreni-duse029.jpg/330px-Kosovo-metohija-koreni-duse029.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Kosovo-metohija-koreni-duse029.jpg/440px-Kosovo-metohija-koreni-duse029.jpg 2x" data-file-width="650" data-file-height="415" /></a><figcaption>Serbian and other children refugees, Cernica, Gjilan.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In some villages under Albanian control in 1998, militants drove ethnic Serbs from their homes.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Some of those who remained are unaccounted for and are presumed to have been abducted by the KLA and killed. The KLA detained an estimated 85 Serbs during its 19 July 1998 attack on <a href="/wiki/Rahovec" title="Rahovec">Rahovec</a>. 35 of these were subsequently released but the others remained. On 22 July 1998, the KLA briefly took control of the Belaćevac mine near the town of Obiliq. Nine Serb mineworkers were captured that day and they remain on the <a href="/wiki/International_Committee_of_the_Red_Cross" title="International Committee of the Red Cross">International Committee of the Red Cross</a>'s list of the missing and are presumed to have been killed.<sup id="cite_ref-hrw.org_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hrw.org-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> In August 1998, 22 Serbian civilians were reportedly killed in the village of Klečka, where the police claimed to have discovered human remains and a kiln used to cremate the bodies.<sup id="cite_ref-hrw.org_112-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hrw.org-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> In September 1998, Serbian police collected 34 bodies of people believed to have been seized and murdered by the KLA, among them some ethnic Albanians, at Lake Radonjić near Glođane (Gllogjan) in what became known as the <a href="/wiki/Lake_Radonji%C4%87_massacre" title="Lake Radonjić massacre">Lake Radonjić massacre</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-hrw.org_112-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hrw.org-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Human Rights Watch have raised questions about the validity of at least some of these allegations made by Serbian authorities.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Monumenti_Heroinat_n%C3%AB_Prishtin%C3%AB.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Monumenti_Heroinat_n%C3%AB_Prishtin%C3%AB.jpg/200px-Monumenti_Heroinat_n%C3%AB_Prishtin%C3%AB.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="280" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Monumenti_Heroinat_n%C3%AB_Prishtin%C3%AB.jpg/300px-Monumenti_Heroinat_n%C3%AB_Prishtin%C3%AB.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Monumenti_Heroinat_n%C3%AB_Prishtin%C3%AB.jpg/400px-Monumenti_Heroinat_n%C3%AB_Prishtin%C3%AB.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2368" data-file-height="3320" /></a><figcaption>"Heroinat" (Heroines) monument in <a href="/wiki/Pristina" title="Pristina">Pristina</a>. It is dedicated to women victims of sexual violence during the Kosovo War, most of whom were Albanian women.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>The International Criminal Tribunal for the former Yugoslavia (ICTY) prosecuted crimes committed during the Kosovo War. Nine senior Yugoslav officials, including Milošević, were indicted for <a href="/wiki/Crime_against_humanity" class="mw-redirect" title="Crime against humanity">crimes against humanity</a> and <a href="/wiki/War_crimes" class="mw-redirect" title="War crimes">war crimes</a> committed between January and June 1999. Six of the defendants were convicted, one was acquitted, one died before his trial could commence, and one (Milošević) died before his trial could conclude.<sup id="cite_ref-icty_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-icty-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> Six KLA members were charged with crimes against humanity and war crimes by the ICTY following the war, and one was convicted.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Haradinaj_cleared_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Haradinaj_cleared-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In total around 10,317 civilians were killed during the war, of whom 8,676 were Albanians, 1,196 Serbs and 445 Roma and others in addition to 3,218 killed members of armed formations.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><br />
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Postwar">Postwar</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Interim_Administration_Mission_in_Kosovo" title="United Nations Interim Administration Mission in Kosovo">United Nations Interim Administration Mission in Kosovo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_status_process" class="mw-redirect" title="Kosovo status process">Status process of Kosovo</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Provisional_Institutions_of_Self-Government" title="Provisional Institutions of Self-Government">Provisional Institutions of Self-Government</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Clintons_visit_Stenkovic_1_Refugee_Camp.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Clintons_visit_Stenkovic_1_Refugee_Camp.jpg/170px-Clintons_visit_Stenkovic_1_Refugee_Camp.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="261" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Clintons_visit_Stenkovic_1_Refugee_Camp.jpg/255px-Clintons_visit_Stenkovic_1_Refugee_Camp.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Clintons_visit_Stenkovic_1_Refugee_Camp.jpg/340px-Clintons_visit_Stenkovic_1_Refugee_Camp.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3126" data-file-height="4800" /></a><figcaption>US President <a href="/wiki/Bill_Clinton" title="Bill Clinton">Bill Clinton</a> with Albanian children during his visit to Kosovo, June 1999.</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 10 June 1999, the UN Security Council passed <a href="/wiki/UN_Security_Council_Resolution_1244" class="mw-redirect" title="UN Security Council Resolution 1244">UN Security Council Resolution 1244</a>, which placed Kosovo under transitional UN administration (UNMIK) and authorised <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Force" title="Kosovo Force">Kosovo Force</a> (KFOR), a NATO-led peacekeeping force. Resolution 1244 provided that Kosovo would have autonomy within the Federal Republic of Yugoslavia, and affirmed the <a href="/wiki/Territorial_integrity" title="Territorial integrity">territorial integrity</a> of Yugoslavia, which has been legally succeeded by the Republic of Serbia.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Estimates of the number of Serbs who left when Serbian forces left Kosovo vary from 65,000<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> to 250,000.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Within post-conflict Kosovo Albanian society, calls for retaliation for previous violence done by Serb forces during the war circulated through public culture.<sup id="cite_ref-Herscher14_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Herscher14-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> Widespread attacks against Serbian cultural sites commenced following the conflict and the return of hundreds of thousands of Kosovo Albanian refugees to their homes.<sup id="cite_ref-Riedlmayer11_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Riedlmayer11-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> In 2004, prolonged negotiations over Kosovo's future status, sociopolitical problems and nationalist sentiments resulted in the <a href="/wiki/2004_unrest_in_Kosovo" title="2004 unrest in Kosovo">Kosovo unrest</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-RauschBanar246_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RauschBanar246-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Egleder79_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Egleder79-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> 11 Albanians and 16 Serbs were killed, 900 people (including peacekeepers) were injured, and several houses, public buildings and churches were damaged or destroyed.
</p><p>International negotiations began in 2006 to determine the final status of Kosovo, as envisaged under <a href="/wiki/UN_Security_Council_Resolution_1244" class="mw-redirect" title="UN Security Council Resolution 1244">UN Security Council Resolution 1244</a>. The UN-backed talks, led by UN <a href="/wiki/Diplomatic_rank" title="Diplomatic rank">Special Envoy</a> <a href="/wiki/Martti_Ahtisaari" title="Martti Ahtisaari">Martti Ahtisaari</a>, began in February 2006. Whilst progress was made on technical matters, both parties remained diametrically opposed on the question of status itself.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In February 2007, Ahtisaari delivered a draft status settlement proposal to leaders in Belgrade and Pristina, the basis for a draft <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution" class="mw-redirect" title="United Nations Security Council Resolution">UN Security Council Resolution</a> which proposed 'supervised independence' for the province. A draft resolution, backed by the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>, the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a> and other European members of the <a href="/wiki/Security_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="Security Council">Security Council</a>, was presented and rewritten four times to try to accommodate Russian concerns that such a resolution would undermine the principle of state sovereignty.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Camp_bondsteel_kosovo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Camp_bondsteel_kosovo.jpg/220px-Camp_bondsteel_kosovo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Camp_bondsteel_kosovo.jpg/330px-Camp_bondsteel_kosovo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Camp_bondsteel_kosovo.jpg/440px-Camp_bondsteel_kosovo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="672" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Camp_Bondsteel" title="Camp Bondsteel">Camp Bondsteel</a> is the main base of the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Army" title="United States Army">United States Army</a> under KFOR command in south-eastern part of Kosovo near the city of <a href="/wiki/Ferizaj" title="Ferizaj">Ferizaj</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Russia, which holds a veto in the Security Council as one of five permanent members, had stated that it would not support any resolution which was not acceptable to both Belgrade and Kosovo Albanians.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> Whilst most observers had, at the beginning of the talks, anticipated independence as the most likely outcome, others have suggested that a rapid resolution might not be preferable.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After many weeks of discussions at the UN, the United States, United Kingdom and other European members of the Security Council formally 'discarded' a draft resolution backing Ahtisaari's proposal on 20 July 2007, having failed to secure Russian backing. Beginning in August, a "<a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/troika" class="extiw" title="wikt:troika">Troika</a>" consisting of negotiators from the European Union (<a href="/wiki/Wolfgang_Ischinger" title="Wolfgang Ischinger">Wolfgang Ischinger</a>), the United States (<a href="/wiki/Frank_G._Wisner" title="Frank G. Wisner">Frank G. Wisner</a>) and Russia (Alexander Botsan-Kharchenko) launched a new effort to reach a status outcome acceptable to both Belgrade and Pristina. Despite Russian disapproval, the U.S., the United Kingdom, and France appeared likely to recognise Kosovar independence.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> A declaration of independence by Kosovar Albanian leaders was postponed until the end of the <a href="/wiki/2008_Serbian_presidential_election" title="2008 Serbian presidential election">Serbian presidential elections</a> (4 February 2008). A significant portion of politicians in both the EU and the US had feared that a premature declaration could boost support in Serbia for the nationalist candidate, <a href="/wiki/Tomislav_Nikoli%C4%87" title="Tomislav Nikolić">Tomislav Nikolić</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In November 2001, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe</a> supervised the <a href="/wiki/2004_Kosovar_parliamentary_election" title="2004 Kosovar parliamentary election">first elections</a> for the <a href="/wiki/Assembly_of_Kosovo" class="mw-redirect" title="Assembly of Kosovo">Assembly of Kosovo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> After that election, Kosovo's political parties formed an all-party unity coalition and elected <a href="/wiki/Ibrahim_Rugova" title="Ibrahim Rugova">Ibrahim Rugova</a> as president and <a href="/wiki/Bajram_Rexhepi" title="Bajram Rexhepi">Bajram Rexhepi</a> (PDK) as Prime Minister.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> After Kosovo-wide elections in October 2004, the LDK and AAK formed a new governing coalition that did not include PDK and Ora. This coalition agreement resulted in <a href="/wiki/Ramush_Haradinaj" title="Ramush Haradinaj">Ramush Haradinaj</a> (AAK) becoming Prime Minister, while Ibrahim Rugova retained the position of President. PDK and Ora were critical of the coalition agreement and have since frequently accused that government of corruption.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/2007_Kosovar_parliamentary_election" title="2007 Kosovar parliamentary election">Parliamentary elections</a> were held on 17 November 2007. After early results, <a href="/wiki/Hashim_Tha%C3%A7i" title="Hashim Thaçi">Hashim Thaçi</a> who was on course to gain 35 per cent of the vote, claimed victory for PDK, the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Kosovo" title="Democratic Party of Kosovo">Democratic Party of Kosovo</a>, and stated his intention to declare independence. Thaçi formed a coalition with current president <a href="/wiki/Fatmir_Sejdiu" title="Fatmir Sejdiu">Fatmir Sejdiu</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Democratic_League_of_Kosovo" title="Democratic League of Kosovo">Democratic League</a> which was in second place with 22 percent of the vote.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> The turnout at the election was particularly low. Most members of the Serb minority refused to vote.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Declaration_of_independence">Declaration of independence</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence" title="2008 Kosovo declaration of independence">2008 Kosovo declaration of independence</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/International_recognition_of_Kosovo" title="International recognition of Kosovo">International recognition of Kosovo</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Geb%C3%A4ude_der_UNMIK_NEW_BORN_SIGN_PRISTINA_KOSOVO_Giv_Owned_Image_23_August_2008.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Geb%C3%A4ude_der_UNMIK_NEW_BORN_SIGN_PRISTINA_KOSOVO_Giv_Owned_Image_23_August_2008.jpg/220px-Geb%C3%A4ude_der_UNMIK_NEW_BORN_SIGN_PRISTINA_KOSOVO_Giv_Owned_Image_23_August_2008.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="122" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Geb%C3%A4ude_der_UNMIK_NEW_BORN_SIGN_PRISTINA_KOSOVO_Giv_Owned_Image_23_August_2008.jpg/330px-Geb%C3%A4ude_der_UNMIK_NEW_BORN_SIGN_PRISTINA_KOSOVO_Giv_Owned_Image_23_August_2008.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Geb%C3%A4ude_der_UNMIK_NEW_BORN_SIGN_PRISTINA_KOSOVO_Giv_Owned_Image_23_August_2008.jpg/440px-Geb%C3%A4ude_der_UNMIK_NEW_BORN_SIGN_PRISTINA_KOSOVO_Giv_Owned_Image_23_August_2008.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3467" data-file-height="1916" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Newborn_monument" title="Newborn monument">Newborn monument</a> unveiled at the celebration of the 2008 Kosovo declaration of independence proclaimed earlier that day, 17 February 2008, <a href="/wiki/Pristina" title="Pristina">Pristina</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Kosovo declared independence from <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a> on 17 February 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_proclaim_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc_proclaim-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> As of&#32;4&#32;September&#32;2020,&#32;<a href="/wiki/International_recognition_of_Kosovo" title="International recognition of Kosovo">114&#160;UN states</a> recognised its independence, including all of its immediate neighbours, with the exception of Serbia;<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> 15 states have subsequently withdrawn that recognition.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> Of the UN Security Council members, while the USA, UK and France do recognise Kosovo's independence, Russia and China do not.<sup id="cite_ref-voa_serbia_kosovo_agreement_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-voa_serbia_kosovo_agreement-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Since declaring independence, it has become a member of international institutions such as the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a> and <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> though not of the United Nations.
</p><p>The Serb minority of Kosovo, which largely opposes the declaration of independence, has formed the <a href="/wiki/Community_of_Serb_Municipalities" title="Community of Serb Municipalities">Community Assembly of Kosovo and Metohija</a> in response. The creation of the assembly was condemned by Kosovo's President Fatmir Sejdiu, while UNMIK has said the assembly is not a serious issue because it will not have an operative role.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
On 8 October 2008, the UN General Assembly resolved, on a proposal by Serbia, to ask the <a href="/wiki/International_Court_of_Justice" title="International Court of Justice">International Court of Justice</a> to render an advisory opinion on the legality of Kosovo's declaration of independence. The advisory opinion, which is not binding over decisions by states to recognise or not recognise Kosovo, was rendered on 22 July 2010, holding that Kosovo's declaration of independence was not in violation either of general principles of international law, which do not prohibit unilateral declarations of independence, nor of specific international law&#160;– in particular UNSCR 1244&#160;– which did not define the final status process nor reserve the outcome to a decision of the Security Council.<sup id="cite_ref-icj-cij_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-icj-cij-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some rapprochement between the two governments took place on 19 April 2013 as both parties reached the <a href="/wiki/Brussels_Agreement_(2013)" title="Brussels Agreement (2013)">Brussels Agreement</a>, an agreement brokered by the EU that allowed the Serb minority in Kosovo to have its own police force and court of appeals.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> The agreement is yet to be ratified by either parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> Presidents of Serbia and Kosovo organized two meetings, in <a href="/wiki/Brussels" title="Brussels">Brussels</a> on 27 February 2023 and <a href="/wiki/Ohrid" title="Ohrid">Ohrid</a> on 18 March 2023, to create and agree upon an 11-point agreement on implementing a European Union-backed deal to normalize ties between the two countries, which includes recognizing "each other's documents such as passports and license plates".<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Governance">Governance</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Kosovo" title="Politics of Kosovo">Politics of Kosovo</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Kosovo" title="Government of Kosovo">Government of Kosovo</a></div>
<table class="wikitable" style="text-align:left; float:right; margin-right:9px; margin-left:2px;">

<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align:left;"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Vjosa_Osmani1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Vjosa_Osmani1.jpg/137px-Vjosa_Osmani1.jpg" decoding="async" width="137" height="171" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Vjosa_Osmani1.jpg/206px-Vjosa_Osmani1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Vjosa_Osmani1.jpg/274px-Vjosa_Osmani1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3726" data-file-height="4657" /></a></span>
</td>
<td style="text-align:left;"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Albin_Kurti_(2023-02-18).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Albin_Kurti_%282023-02-18%29.jpg/123px-Albin_Kurti_%282023-02-18%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="123" height="171" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Albin_Kurti_%282023-02-18%29.jpg/185px-Albin_Kurti_%282023-02-18%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Albin_Kurti_%282023-02-18%29.jpg/246px-Albin_Kurti_%282023-02-18%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="750" data-file-height="1040" /></a></span>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Vjosa_Osmani" title="Vjosa Osmani">Vjosa Osmani</a><br /><span style="font-size:85%;"><a href="/wiki/President_of_Kosovo" title="President of Kosovo">President</a></span>
</td>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Albin_Kurti" title="Albin Kurti">Albin Kurti</a><br /><span style="font-size:85%;"><a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Kosovo" title="Prime Minister of Kosovo">Prime Minister</a></span>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Kosovo is a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party</a> <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democratic</a> <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_republic" title="Parliamentary republic">republic</a>. It is governed by <a href="/wiki/Legislative" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative">legislative</a>, <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">executive</a> and <a href="/wiki/Judiciary" title="Judiciary">judicial</a> institutions, which derive from the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Kosovo" title="Constitution of Kosovo">constitution</a>, although, until the <a href="/wiki/Brussels_Agreement_(2013)" title="Brussels Agreement (2013)">Brussels Agreement</a>, North Kosovo was in practice largely controlled by institutions of Serbia or parallel institutions funded by Serbia. Legislative functions are vested in both the <a href="/wiki/Assembly_of_Kosovo" class="mw-redirect" title="Assembly of Kosovo">Parliament</a> and the ministers within their competencies. The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Kosovo" title="Government of Kosovo">Government</a> exercises the executive power and is composed of the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Kosovo" title="Prime Minister of Kosovo">Prime Minister</a> as the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>, the Deputy Prime Ministers and the Ministers of the various ministries.
</p><p>The judiciary is composed of the Supreme Court and subordinate courts, a <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Kosovo" title="Constitutional Court of Kosovo">Constitutional Court</a>, and independent prosecutorial institutions. There also exist multiple independent institutions defined by the constitution and law, as well as local governments. All citizens are equal before the law and <a href="/wiki/Gender_equality" title="Gender equality">gender equality</a> is ensured by the constitution.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceB_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceB-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> The Constitutional Framework guarantees a minimum of ten seats in the 120-member Assembly for Serbs, and ten for other minorities, and also guarantees Serbs and other minorities places in the Government.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/President_of_Kosovo" title="President of Kosovo">president</a> serves as the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> and represents the unity of the people, elected every five years, indirectly by the parliament through a <a href="/wiki/Secret_ballot" title="Secret ballot">secret ballot</a> by a two-thirds majority of all deputies. The head of state is invested primarily with representative responsibilities and powers. The president has the power to return draft legislation to the parliament for reconsideration and has a role in foreign affairs and certain official appointments.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Kosovo" title="Prime Minister of Kosovo">Prime Minister</a> serves as the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a> elected by the parliament. Ministers are nominated by the Prime Minister, and then confirmed by the parliament. The head of government exercises executive power of the territory.
</p><p>Corruption is a major problem and an obstacle to the development of democracy in the country. Those in the judiciary appointed by the government to fight corruption are often government associates. Moreover, prominent politicians and party operatives who commit offences are not prosecuted due to the lack of laws and political will. Organized crime also poses a threat to the economy due to the practices of bribery, extortion and racketeering.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Kosovo" title="Foreign relations of Kosovo">Foreign relations of Kosovo</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Kosovo" title="Foreign relations of Kosovo">foreign relations of Kosovo</a> are conducted through the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Kosovo)" title="Ministry of Foreign Affairs (Kosovo)">Ministry of Foreign Affairs</a> in <a href="/wiki/Pristina" title="Pristina">Pristina</a>. As of 2023<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kosovo&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 101 out of 193 <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> member states <a href="/wiki/International_recognition_of_Kosovo" title="International recognition of Kosovo">recognise</a> the Republic of Kosovo. Within the <a href="/wiki/European_Union_member_states" class="mw-redirect" title="European Union member states">European Union</a>, it is recognized by 22 of 27 members and is a <a href="/wiki/Accession_of_Kosovo_to_the_European_Union" title="Accession of Kosovo to the European Union">potential candidate</a> for the <a href="/wiki/Future_enlargement_of_the_European_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Future enlargement of the European Union">future enlargement of the European Union</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> On 15 December 2022 Kosovo filed a formal application to become a member of the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-dw10_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dw10-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Kosovo is a member of several international organizations including the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a>, <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Road_and_Transport_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="International Road and Transport Union">International Road and Transport Union</a>, Regional Cooperation Council, <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Europe_Development_Bank" title="Council of Europe Development Bank">Council of Europe Development Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/Venice_Commission" title="Venice Commission">Venice Commission</a> and <a href="/wiki/European_Bank_for_Reconstruction_and_Development" title="European Bank for Reconstruction and Development">European Bank for Reconstruction and Development</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Will_the_EBRD_do_the_right_thing?_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Will_the_EBRD_do_the_right_thing?-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> In 2015, Kosovo's bid to become a member of <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a> fell three votes short of the two-thirds majority required to join.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> 23 countries maintain <a href="/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_Kosovo" title="List of diplomatic missions in Kosovo">embassies</a> in Kosovo.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Kosovo maintains 24 <a href="/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_Kosovo" title="List of diplomatic missions of Kosovo">diplomatic missions</a> and 28 consular missions abroad.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Albania%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations" title="Albania–Kosovo relations">relations with Albania</a> are in a special case considering that both countries share the same language and culture. The <a href="/wiki/Albanian_language" title="Albanian language">Albanian language</a> is one of the <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Kosovo" class="mw-redirect" title="Languages of Kosovo">official languages</a> of Kosovo. <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a> has an embassy in the capital <a href="/wiki/Pristina" title="Pristina">Pristina</a> and Kosovo an embassy in <a href="/wiki/Tirana" title="Tirana">Tirana</a>. In 1992, Albania was the only country whose parliament voted to recognise the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Kosova" title="Republic of Kosova">Republic of Kosova</a>. <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a> was also one of the first countries to officially announce its recognition of the Republic of Kosovo in February 2008.
</p><p>From 1 January 2024 Kosovo nationals will be exempt from visa requirements within the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Area" title="Schengen Area">Schengen Area</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Law">Law</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Law_of_Kosovo" title="Law of Kosovo">Law of Kosovo</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:KP-PK_During_Kosovo_Independence_Parade.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/KP-PK_During_Kosovo_Independence_Parade.JPG/220px-KP-PK_During_Kosovo_Independence_Parade.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/KP-PK_During_Kosovo_Independence_Parade.JPG/330px-KP-PK_During_Kosovo_Independence_Parade.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/KP-PK_During_Kosovo_Independence_Parade.JPG/440px-KP-PK_During_Kosovo_Independence_Parade.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2496" data-file-height="1664" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Police" title="Kosovo Police">Kosovo Police</a> is the main law enforcement agency in Kosovo.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Judiciary_of_Kosovo" title="Judiciary of Kosovo">judicial system of Kosovo</a> follows a <a href="/wiki/Civil_law_(legal_system)" title="Civil law (legal system)">civil law</a> framework and comprises regular civil and criminal courts, alongside administrative courts. Administered by the <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Judicial_Council" title="Kosovo Judicial Council">judicial council</a> in Pristina, the system includes the supreme court as the highest judicial authority, a <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Kosovo" title="Constitutional Court of Kosovo">constitutional court</a> and an independent prosecutorial institution. Following the independence of Kosovo in 2008, the <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Police" title="Kosovo Police">Kosovo Police</a> assumed the primary law enforcement responsibilities within the country.
</p><p>Covering a broad range of issues related to the status of Kosovo, the <a href="/wiki/Ahtisaari_Plan" title="Ahtisaari Plan">Ahtisaari Plan</a> introduced two forms of international supervision for Kosovo following its independence, including the <a href="/wiki/International_Civilian_Office" class="mw-redirect" title="International Civilian Office">International Civilian Office</a> (ICO) and the <a href="/wiki/EULEX" class="mw-redirect" title="EULEX">European Union Rule of Law Mission to Kosovo</a> (EULEX).<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> The ICO monitored plan implementation and possessed veto powers, while EULEX focused on developing judicial systems and had arrest and prosecution authority. These bodies were granted powers under Kosovo's declaration of independence and constitution.
</p><p>The legal status of the ICO depended upon the de facto situation and Kosovo legislation, with oversight provided by the <a href="/wiki/International_Steering_Group" class="mw-redirect" title="International Steering Group">International Steering Group</a> (ISG) comprising states that recognized Kosovo. Serbia and non-recognizing states did not acknowledge the ICO. Despite initial opposition, EULEX gained acceptance from Serbia and the UN Security Council in 2008. It operated under the UNMIK mandate with operational independence. The ICO concluded operations in 2012 after fulfilling obligations, while EULEX continues to operate within Kosovo and international law. Its role has been extended, primarily focusing on monitoring with reduced responsibilities.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Security_Force" title="Kosovo Security Force">Kosovo Security Force</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kosovo_Security_Force_FSK-KSF.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Kosovo_Security_Force_FSK-KSF.jpg/220px-Kosovo_Security_Force_FSK-KSF.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="172" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Kosovo_Security_Force_FSK-KSF.jpg/330px-Kosovo_Security_Force_FSK-KSF.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Kosovo_Security_Force_FSK-KSF.jpg/440px-Kosovo_Security_Force_FSK-KSF.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1308" data-file-height="1020" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Security_Force" title="Kosovo Security Force">Kosovo Security Force</a> is the military of Kosovo.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Security_Force" title="Kosovo Security Force">Kosovo Security Force</a> (KSF) is the national security force of Kosovo commissioned with the task of preserving and safeguarding the country's territorial integrity, national sovereignty and the security interests of its population.<sup id="cite_ref-KSF_166-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KSF-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Functioning under the president of Kosovo as the <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-chief" title="Commander-in-chief">commander-in-chief</a>, the security force adheres to the principle of non-discrimination, guaranteeing equal protection for its personnel regardless of gender or ethnicity.<sup id="cite_ref-KSF_166-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KSF-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Kosovo's notable challenges are identified in the realms of persistent conflicts and societal safety and security, both of which are intertwined with the country's diplomatic ties to neighboring countries and its domestic social and political stability.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2008, under the leadership of NATO, the Kosovo Force (KFOR) and the <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Protection_Corps" title="Kosovo Protection Corps">Kosovo Protection Corps</a> (KPC) undertook preparations for the formation of the Kosovo Security Force. A significant milestone occurred in 2014 when the government officially announced its decision to establish a Ministry of Defense in 2019, with the aim of transforming the existing Kosovo Security Force into the Kosovo Armed Forces. This transformation would entail aligning the armed forces with the high standards expected of NATO members, reflecting Kosovo's aspiration to join the alliance in the future.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> Subsequently, in December 2018, the government enacted legislation to redefine the mandate of the Kosovo Security Force, effecting its transformation into anarmy. Concurrently, the establishment of a Ministry of Defense was set in motion, further solidifying these developments and ensuring the necessary infrastructure and oversight for the newly formed armed forces.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Kosovo" title="Districts of Kosovo">Districts of Kosovo</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_Kosovo" title="Municipalities of Kosovo">Municipalities of Kosovo</a></div>
<p>Kosovo is divided into seven <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Kosovo" title="Districts of Kosovo">districts</a> (<a href="/wiki/Albanian_language" title="Albanian language">Albanian</a>: <i lang="sq">rajon</i>; <a href="/wiki/Gaj%27s_Latin_alphabet" title="Gaj&#39;s Latin alphabet">Serbian</a>: <i lang="sr-Latn">okrug</i>), according to the Law of Kosovo and the Brussels Agreement of 2013, which stipulated the formation of new municipalities with Serb majority populations. The districts are further subdivided into 38 <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_Kosovo" title="Municipalities of Kosovo">municipalities</a> (<span title="Albanian-language text"><i lang="sq">komunë</i></span>; <span title="Serbian-language text"><i lang="sr-Latn">opština</i></span>). The largest and most populous district of Kosovo is the <a href="/wiki/District_of_Pristina" title="District of Pristina">District of Pristina</a> with the capital in <a href="/wiki/Pristina" title="Pristina">Pristina</a>, having a surface area of 2,470&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (953.67&#160;sq&#160;mi) and a population of 477,312.
</p>
<table align="center" style="background:none;" cellspacing="3px">

<tbody><tr style="font-size:98%; text-align:center;">

<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Kosovo" title="Geography of Kosovo">Geography of Kosovo</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dromodol_hill_in_Rugova_-_Paradise_Lost.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Dromodol_hill_in_Rugova_-_Paradise_Lost.jpg/220px-Dromodol_hill_in_Rugova_-_Paradise_Lost.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Dromodol_hill_in_Rugova_-_Paradise_Lost.jpg/330px-Dromodol_hill_in_Rugova_-_Paradise_Lost.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Dromodol_hill_in_Rugova_-_Paradise_Lost.jpg/440px-Dromodol_hill_in_Rugova_-_Paradise_Lost.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2160" data-file-height="1440" /></a><figcaption>Landscape in <a href="/wiki/Rugova_(region)" title="Rugova (region)">Rugova</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Bjeshk%C3%ABt_e_Nemuna_National_Park" title="Bjeshkët e Nemuna National Park">Bjeshkët e Nemuna National Park</a> bordering <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Defined in a total area of 10,887 square kilometres (4,203 square miles), Kosovo is <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked</a> and located in the center of the <a href="/wiki/Balkan_Peninsula" class="mw-redirect" title="Balkan Peninsula">Balkan Peninsula</a> in <a href="/wiki/Southeastern_Europe" class="mw-redirect" title="Southeastern Europe">Southeastern Europe</a>. It lies between latitudes <a href="/wiki/42nd_parallel_north" title="42nd parallel north">42°</a> and <a href="/wiki/43rd_parallel_north" title="43rd parallel north">43° N</a>, and longitudes <a href="/wiki/20th_meridian_east" title="20th meridian east">20°</a> and <a href="/wiki/22nd_meridian_east" title="22nd meridian east">22° E</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> The northernmost point is Bellobërda at 43° 14' 06" northern latitude; the southernmost is Restelica at 41° 56' 40" northern latitude; the westernmost point is <a href="/wiki/Bog%C3%AB,_Kosovo" title="Bogë, Kosovo">Bogë</a> at 20° 3' 23" eastern longitude; and the easternmost point is <a href="/wiki/Desivojca" title="Desivojca">Desivojca</a> at 21° 44' 21" eastern longitude. The highest point is <a href="/wiki/Velika_Rudoka" title="Velika Rudoka">Velika Rudoka</a> at 2,658 metres (8,720&#160;ft) <a href="/wiki/Metres_above_sea_level" class="mw-redirect" title="Metres above sea level">above sea level</a>, and the lowest is the <a href="/wiki/White_Drin" title="White Drin">White Drin</a> at 297 metres (974&#160;ft).
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Brod_Dragash.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Brod_Dragash.jpg/220px-Brod_Dragash.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Brod_Dragash.jpg/330px-Brod_Dragash.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Brod_Dragash.jpg/440px-Brod_Dragash.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4256" data-file-height="2394" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0ar_Mountains" title="Šar Mountains">Šar Mountains</a> encompass one-tenth of Kosovo's territory.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>Most of the borders of Kosovo are dominated by mountainous and high terrain. The most noticeable <a href="/wiki/Topography" title="Topography">topographical</a> features are the <a href="/wiki/Accursed_Mountains" title="Accursed Mountains">Accursed Mountains</a> and the <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0ar_Mountains" title="Šar Mountains">Šar Mountains</a>. The Accursed Mountains are a geological continuation of the <a href="/wiki/Dinaric_Alps" title="Dinaric Alps">Dinaric Alps</a>. The mountains run laterally through the west along the border with <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a> and <a href="/wiki/Montenegro" title="Montenegro">Montenegro</a>. The southeast is predominantly the Šar Mountains, which constitute the border with <a href="/wiki/North_Macedonia" title="North Macedonia">North Macedonia</a>. Besides the mountain ranges, Kosovo's territory consists mostly of two major plains, the <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Plain" class="mw-redirect" title="Kosovo Plain">Kosovo Plain</a> in the east and the <a href="/wiki/Metohija" title="Metohija">Metohija Plain</a> in the west.
</p><p>Additionally, Kosovo consists of multiple geographic and ethnographic regions, such as <a href="/wiki/Drenica" title="Drenica">Drenica</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dushkaja" title="Dushkaja">Dushkaja</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gollak" title="Gollak">Gollak</a>, <a href="/wiki/Has_(region)" title="Has (region)">Has</a>, <a href="/wiki/Highlands_of_Gjakova" title="Highlands of Gjakova">Highlands of Gjakova</a>, <a href="/wiki/Llap_(region)" title="Llap (region)">Llap</a>, <a href="/wiki/Llapusha" title="Llapusha">Llapusha</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rugova_(region)" title="Rugova (region)">Rugova</a>.
</p><p>Kosovo's hydrological resources are relatively small; there are few <a href="/wiki/Lake" title="Lake">lakes</a> in Kosovo, the largest of which are <a href="/wiki/Batllava_Lake" title="Batllava Lake">Batllava Lake</a>, <a href="/wiki/Badovc_Lake" title="Badovc Lake">Badovc Lake</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lake_Gazivoda" class="mw-redirect" title="Lake Gazivoda">Lake Gazivoda</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lake_Radoniq" class="mw-redirect" title="Lake Radoniq">Lake Radoniq</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> In addition to these, Kosovo also does have <a href="/wiki/Karst_springs" class="mw-redirect" title="Karst springs">karst springs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Thermal_water" class="mw-redirect" title="Thermal water">thermal</a> and mineral water springs.<sup id="cite_ref-ICMMt_175-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ICMMt-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> The longest rivers of Kosovo include the <a href="/wiki/White_Drin" title="White Drin">White Drin</a>, the <a href="/wiki/South_Morava" title="South Morava">South Morava</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Ibar_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Ibar River">Ibar</a>. <a href="/wiki/Sitnica" title="Sitnica">Sitnica</a>, a tributary of Ibar, is the largest river lying completely within Kosovo's territory. River <a href="/wiki/Nerodimka_(river)" class="mw-redirect" title="Nerodimka (river)">Nerodimka</a> represents Europe's only instance of a river bifurcation flowing into the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a> and <a href="/wiki/Aegean_Sea" title="Aegean Sea">Aegean Sea</a>.
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Kosovo" title="Climate of Kosovo">Climate of Kosovo</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ost%C3%ABrvice_from_Brezovica.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Ost%C3%ABrvice_from_Brezovica.jpg/220px-Ost%C3%ABrvice_from_Brezovica.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Ost%C3%ABrvice_from_Brezovica.jpg/330px-Ost%C3%ABrvice_from_Brezovica.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Ost%C3%ABrvice_from_Brezovica.jpg/440px-Ost%C3%ABrvice_from_Brezovica.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2160" data-file-height="1440" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Alpine_climate" title="Alpine climate">Alpine climate</a> in <a href="/wiki/Pashallora" title="Pashallora">Pashallora</a> as seen from <a href="/wiki/Brezovica,_Kosovo" title="Brezovica, Kosovo">Brezovica</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Most of Kosovo experiences predominantly a <a href="/wiki/Continental_climate" title="Continental climate">Continental climate</a> with <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_climate" title="Mediterranean climate">Mediterranean</a> and <a href="/wiki/Alpine_climate" title="Alpine climate">Alpine</a> influences,<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> strongly influenced by Kosovo's proximity to the <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Sea" title="Adriatic Sea">Adriatic Sea</a> in the west, the <a href="/wiki/Aegean_Sea" title="Aegean Sea">Aegean Sea</a> in the south as well as the European continental landmass in the north.<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The coldest areas are situated in the mountainous region to the west and southeast, where an Alpine climate is prevalent. The warmest areas are mostly in the extreme southern areas close to the border with Albania, where a Mediterranean climate is the norm. Mean monthly temperature ranges between 0&#160;<a href="/wiki/Celsius" title="Celsius">°C</a> (32&#160;<a href="/wiki/Fahrenheit" title="Fahrenheit">°F</a>) (in January) and 22&#160;°C (72&#160;°F) (in July). Mean annual precipitation ranges from 600 to 1,300&#160;mm (24 to 51&#160;in) per year, and is well distributed year-round.
</p><p>To the northeast, the <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Plain" class="mw-redirect" title="Kosovo Plain">Kosovo Plain</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ibar_(river)" title="Ibar (river)">Ibar Valley</a> are drier with total precipitation of about 600 millimetres (24 inches) per year and more influenced by continental air masses, with colder winters and very hot summers. In the southwest, climatic area of <a href="/wiki/Metohija" title="Metohija">Metohija</a> receives more mediterranean influences with warmer summers, somewhat higher precipitation (700&#160;mm (28&#160;in)) and heavy snowfalls in the winter. The mountainous areas of the <a href="/wiki/Accursed_Mountains" title="Accursed Mountains">Accursed Mountains</a> in the west, <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0ar_Mountains" title="Šar Mountains">Šar Mountains</a> on the south and <a href="/wiki/Kopaonik" title="Kopaonik">Kopaonik</a> in the north experiences alpine climate, with high precipitation (900 to 1,300&#160;mm (35 to 51&#160;in) per year), short and fresh summers, and cold winters.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> The average annual temperature of Kosovo is 9.5&#160;°C (49.1&#160;°F). The warmest month is July with average temperature of 19.2&#160;°C (66.6&#160;°F), and the coldest is January with −1.3&#160;°C (29.7&#160;°F). Except <a href="/wiki/Prizren" title="Prizren">Prizren</a> and <a href="/wiki/Istog" title="Istog">Istog</a>, all other meteorological stations in January recorded average temperatures under 0&#160;°C (32&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity">Biodiversity</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity_of_Kosovo" title="Biodiversity of Kosovo">Biodiversity of Kosovo</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Kosovo" title="Protected areas of Kosovo">Protected areas of Kosovo</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Parku_Komb%C3%ABtar_Bjeshk%C3%ABt_Nemuna,_Liqeni_i_madh_ne_Liqenat,_Rugove.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Parku_Komb%C3%ABtar_Bjeshk%C3%ABt_Nemuna%2C_Liqeni_i_madh_ne_Liqenat%2C_Rugove.jpg/220px-Parku_Komb%C3%ABtar_Bjeshk%C3%ABt_Nemuna%2C_Liqeni_i_madh_ne_Liqenat%2C_Rugove.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Parku_Komb%C3%ABtar_Bjeshk%C3%ABt_Nemuna%2C_Liqeni_i_madh_ne_Liqenat%2C_Rugove.jpg/330px-Parku_Komb%C3%ABtar_Bjeshk%C3%ABt_Nemuna%2C_Liqeni_i_madh_ne_Liqenat%2C_Rugove.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Parku_Komb%C3%ABtar_Bjeshk%C3%ABt_Nemuna%2C_Liqeni_i_madh_ne_Liqenat%2C_Rugove.jpg/440px-Parku_Komb%C3%ABtar_Bjeshk%C3%ABt_Nemuna%2C_Liqeni_i_madh_ne_Liqenat%2C_Rugove.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4914" data-file-height="3255" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Bjeshk%C3%ABt_e_Nemuna_National_Park" title="Bjeshkët e Nemuna National Park">Bjeshkët e Nemuna National Park</a> is home to a wide range of flora and fauna species.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Located in <a href="/wiki/Southeastern_Europe" class="mw-redirect" title="Southeastern Europe">Southeastern Europe</a>, Kosovo receives floral and faunal species from Europe and <a href="/wiki/Eurasia" title="Eurasia">Eurasia</a>. Forests are widespread in Kosovo and cover at least 39% of the region. <a href="/wiki/Phytogeographically" class="mw-redirect" title="Phytogeographically">Phytogeographically</a>, it straddles the <a href="/wiki/Illyria" title="Illyria">Illyrian</a> province of the <a href="/wiki/Circumboreal_Region" title="Circumboreal Region">Circumboreal Region</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Boreal_Kingdom" title="Boreal Kingdom">Boreal Kingdom</a>. In addition, it falls within three terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Balkan_mixed_forests" title="Balkan mixed forests">Balkan mixed forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dinaric_Mountains_mixed_forests" title="Dinaric Mountains mixed forests">Dinaric Mountains mixed forests</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pindus_Mountains_mixed_forests" title="Pindus Mountains mixed forests">Pindus Mountains mixed forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_180-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> Kosovo's biodiversity is conserved in two <a href="/wiki/National_park" title="National park">national parks</a>, eleven <a href="/wiki/Nature_reserve" title="Nature reserve">nature reserves</a> and one hundred three other protected areas.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Bjeshk%C3%ABt_e_Nemuna_National_Park" title="Bjeshkët e Nemuna National Park">Bjeshkët e Nemuna National Park</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sharr_Mountains_National_Park_(Kosovo)" class="mw-redirect" title="Sharr Mountains National Park (Kosovo)">Sharr Mountains National Park</a> are the most important regions of vegetation and biodiversity in Kosovo.<sup id="cite_ref-Maxhuni_182-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Maxhuni-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> Kosovo had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 5.19/10, ranking it 107th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_183-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-183">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Flora" title="Flora">Flora</a> encompasses more than 1,800 species of <a href="/wiki/Vascular_plant" title="Vascular plant">vascular plant</a> species, but the actual number is estimated to be higher than 2,500 species.<sup id="cite_ref-KOSOVObio_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KOSOVObio-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> The diversity is the result of the complex interaction of geology and hydrology creating a wide variety of habitat conditions for flora growth. Although, Kosovo represents only 2.3% of the entire surface area of the <a href="/wiki/Balkans" title="Balkans">Balkans</a>, in terms of vegetation it has 25% of the Balkan flora and about 18% of the European flora.<sup id="cite_ref-KOSOVObio_184-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KOSOVObio-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> The fauna is composed of a wide range of species.<sup id="cite_ref-Maxhuni_182-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Maxhuni-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 14">&#58;&#8202;14&#8202;</span></sup> The mountainous west and southeast provide a great habitat for several <a href="/wiki/Rare_species" title="Rare species">rare</a> or <a href="/wiki/Endangered_species" title="Endangered species">endangered species</a> including <a href="/wiki/Brown_bear" title="Brown bear">brown bears</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lynx" title="Lynx">lynxes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wild_cat" class="mw-redirect" title="Wild cat">wild cats</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wolve" class="mw-redirect" title="Wolve">wolves</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fox" title="Fox">foxes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wild_goat" title="Wild goat">wild goats</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roe_deer" title="Roe deer">roebucks</a> and <a href="/wiki/Deer" title="Deer">deers</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> A total of 255 species of <a href="/wiki/Bird" title="Bird">birds</a> have been recorded, with raptors such as the <a href="/wiki/Golden_eagle" title="Golden eagle">golden eagle</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eastern_imperial_eagle" title="Eastern imperial eagle">eastern imperial eagle</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lesser_kestrel" title="Lesser kestrel">lesser kestrel</a> living principally in the mountains of Kosovo.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environmental_issues">Environmental issues</span></h3>
<p>Environmental issues in Kosovo include a wide range of challenges pertaining to <a href="/wiki/Air_pollution" title="Air pollution">air</a> and <a href="/wiki/Water_pollution" title="Water pollution">water pollution</a>, <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_Kosovo" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate change in Kosovo">climate change</a>, <a href="/wiki/Waste_management" title="Waste management">waste management</a>, <a href="/wiki/Conservation_biology" title="Conservation biology">biodiversity loss</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nature_conservation" title="Nature conservation">nature conservation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-EIKOS_187-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EIKOS-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> The vulnerability of the country to climate change is influenced by various factors, such as increased temperatures, geological and hydrological <a href="/wiki/Hazard" title="Hazard">hazards</a>, including droughts, flooding, fires and rains.<sup id="cite_ref-EIKOS_187-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EIKOS-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> Due to its current status, Kosovo is not a signatory to the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Framework_Convention_on_Climate_Change" title="United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change">United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change</a> (UNFCCC), the <a href="/wiki/Kyoto_Protocol" title="Kyoto Protocol">Kyoto Protocol</a> or the <a href="/wiki/Paris_Agreement" title="Paris Agreement">Paris Agreement</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-KOSCP_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KOSCP-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> Consequently, the country is not mandated to submit a <a href="/wiki/Nationally_determined_contribution" title="Nationally determined contribution">Nationally Determined Contribution</a> (NDC) that are voluntary commitments outlining a nation's actions and strategies for mitigating climate change and adapting to its impacts.<sup id="cite_ref-KOSCP_188-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KOSCP-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> However, since 2021, Kosovo is actively engaged in the process of formulating a voluntary NDC, with assistance provided from Japan.<sup id="cite_ref-KOSCP_188-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KOSCP-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-IMFKOS_189-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IMFKOS-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> In 2023, the country has established a goal of reducing <a href="/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions" title="Greenhouse gas emissions">greenhouse gas emissions</a> by approximately 16.3% as part of its broader objective to achieve <a href="/wiki/Carbon_neutrality" class="mw-redirect" title="Carbon neutrality">carbon neutrality</a> by the year 2050.<sup id="cite_ref-IMFKOS_189-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IMFKOS-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Kosovo" title="Demographics of Kosovo">Demographics of Kosovo</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Kosovo" class="mw-redirect" title="Languages of Kosovo">Languages of Kosovo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Demographic_history_of_Kosovo" title="Demographic history of Kosovo">Demographic history of Kosovo</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Population_of_Kosovo_(1921-2015).png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Population_of_Kosovo_%281921-2015%29.png/280px-Population_of_Kosovo_%281921-2015%29.png" decoding="async" width="280" height="158" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Population_of_Kosovo_%281921-2015%29.png/420px-Population_of_Kosovo_%281921-2015%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Population_of_Kosovo_%281921-2015%29.png/560px-Population_of_Kosovo_%281921-2015%29.png 2x" data-file-width="1502" data-file-height="846" /></a><figcaption>The population of Kosovo from 1921 to 2015.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Agency_of_Statistics" title="Kosovo Agency of Statistics">Agency of Statistics</a> estimated Kosovo's population in 2021 to be approximately 1,774,000.<sup id="cite_ref-Chapter_1_190-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chapter_1-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> In 2023, the overall <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_life_expectancy" title="List of countries by life expectancy">life expectancy</a> at birth is 79.68 years; 77.38 years for males and 81.87 years for females.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> The estimated <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> in 2023 is 1.88 children born per woman.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_fb2023_192-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia_fb2023-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> The country is the <a href="/wiki/Balkans#Demographics" title="Balkans">11th most populous country</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Balkans" title="Balkans">Balkans</a> and ranks as the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population" title="List of countries and dependencies by population">148th most populous country</a> in the world. The country's population rose steadily over the 20th century and peaked at an estimated 2.2 million in 1998. The <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_War" title="Kosovo War">Kosovo War</a> and subsequent migration have decreased the population of Kosovo over time.  
</p><p>In 2019, <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Albanians" title="Kosovo Albanians">Albanians</a> constituted 92% of the population of Kosovo, followed by ethnic <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Serbs" title="Kosovo Serbs">Serbs</a> (4%), <a href="/wiki/Bosniaks_in_Kosovo" title="Bosniaks in Kosovo">Bosniaks</a> (2%), <a href="/wiki/Turks_in_Kosovo" title="Turks in Kosovo">Turks</a> (1%), <a href="/wiki/Romani_people_in_Kosovo" title="Romani people in Kosovo">Romani</a> (1%), and the <a href="/wiki/Gorani_people" title="Gorani people">Gorani</a> (&lt;1%).<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> Albanians constitute the majority of the population in most of Kosovo. Ethnic Serbs are concentrated in <a href="/wiki/North_Kosovo" title="North Kosovo">the north</a> of the country, as well as in <a href="/wiki/Serbian_enclaves_in_Kosovo" title="Serbian enclaves in Kosovo">other municipalities</a> in the east of the country, such as <a href="/wiki/Gra%C4%8Danica,_Kosovo" title="Gračanica, Kosovo">Gračanica</a> and <a href="/wiki/%C5%A0trpce" title="Štrpce">Štrpce</a>. Turks form a local majority in the municipality of <a href="/wiki/Mamusha" title="Mamusha">Mamusha</a>, just north of Prizren, while the Bosniaks are mainly located within Prizren itself. The Gorani are concentrated in the southernmost tip of the country, in <a href="/wiki/Dragash" title="Dragash">Dragash</a>. The Romani are spread across the entire country. 
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Official_language" title="Official language">official languages</a> of Kosovo are <a href="/wiki/Albanian_language" title="Albanian language">Albanian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Serbian_language" title="Serbian language">Serbian</a> and the institutions are committed to ensure the equal use of those two languages.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> Municipal civil servants are only required to speak one of the two languages in a professional setting and, according to Language Commissioner of Kosovo Slaviša Mladenović, no government organisation has all of its documents available in both languages.<sup id="cite_ref-ECMI-Kosovo_Language_Commissioner_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ECMI-Kosovo_Language_Commissioner-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> The Law on the Use of Languages gives <a href="/wiki/Turkish_language" title="Turkish language">Turkish</a> the status of an official language in the municipality of <a href="/wiki/Prizren" title="Prizren">Prizren</a>, irrespective of the size of the <a href="/wiki/Turks_of_Kosovo" class="mw-redirect" title="Turks of Kosovo">Turkish community</a> living there.<sup id="cite_ref-COE-Municipalities_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-COE-Municipalities-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> Otherwise, <a href="/wiki/Turkish_language" title="Turkish language">Turkish</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bosnian_language" title="Bosnian language">Bosnian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Romani_language" title="Romani language">Roma</a> hold the status of official languages at municipal level if the linguistic community represents at least 5% of the total population of municipality.<sup id="cite_ref-langauges_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-langauges-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-COE-Municipalities_196-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-COE-Municipalities-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> Albanian is spoken as a <a href="/wiki/First_language" title="First language">first language</a> by all Albanians, as well as some of the Romani people, such as the <a href="/wiki/Ashkali_and_Balkan_Egyptians" title="Ashkali and Balkan Egyptians">Ashkali and Balkan Egyptians</a>. Serbian, Bosnian, and Turkish are spoken as first languages by their respective communities.
</p>

<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Minorities">Minorities</span></h3>
<p>The relations between <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Albanians" title="Kosovo Albanians">Kosovar Albanians</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Serbs" title="Kosovo Serbs">Kosovar Serbs</a> have been hostile since the rise of nationalism in the Balkans during the 19th century.<sup id="cite_ref-Schabnel,_Albrecht_2001._Pp._20_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schabnel,_Albrecht_2001._Pp._20-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> During Communism in Yugoslavia, the ethnic Albanians and Serbs were strongly irreconcilable, with sociological studies during the Tito-era indicating that ethnic Albanians and Serbs rarely accepted each other as neighbors or friends and few held inter-ethnic marriages.<sup id="cite_ref-Schabnel,_Albrecht_2001._Pp._24_200-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schabnel,_Albrecht_2001._Pp._24-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> Ethnic prejudices, stereotypes and mutual distrust between ethnic Albanians and Serbs have remained common for decades.<sup id="cite_ref-Schabnel,_Albrecht_2001._Pp._24_200-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schabnel,_Albrecht_2001._Pp._24-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> The level of intolerance and separation between both communities during the Tito-period was reported by sociologists to be worse than that of Croat and Serb communities in Yugoslavia, which also had tensions but held some closer relations between each other.<sup id="cite_ref-Schabnel,_Albrecht_2001._Pp._24_200-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schabnel,_Albrecht_2001._Pp._24-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite their planned integration into the Kosovar society and their recognition in the Kosovar constitution, the <a href="/wiki/Romani_people" title="Romani people">Romani</a>, Ashkali, and Egyptian communities continue to face many difficulties, such as segregation and discrimination, in housing, education, health, employment and social welfare.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> Many camps around Kosovo continue to house thousands of <a href="/wiki/Internally_displaced_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Internally displaced people">internally displaced people</a>, all of whom are from minority groups and communities.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> Because many of the Roma are believed to have sided with the Serbs during the conflict, taking part in the widespread looting and destruction of Albanian property, <a href="/wiki/Minority_Rights_Group_International" title="Minority Rights Group International">Minority Rights Group International</a> report that Romani people encounter hostility by Albanians outside their local areas.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> A 2020 research report funded by the EU shows that there is a limited scale of trust and overall contact between the major ethnic groups in Kosovo.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
